### U-022-106
- **tipo:** historia
- **titulo:** The author's tutorial hell: seven failed "business models" that were really hobbies, because he never got a customer.
- **desarrollo:** "I got stuck in tutorial hell. I took all the courses. I tried and failed at seven different business models. Facebook ads, SEO, drop shipping, web design, digital art and a few more." He wouldn't call them businesses but hobbies: "I would learn the high value skill and that felt good. I would start the LLC and that felt good. I would build the website and try to write the copy… and that felt good. But when it came to actually getting a customer, getting someone to pay me, I was nowhere to be found." "The missing piece was a strategy, but not just a strategy, the ability to think strategically because how else are you going to create a strategy?" He admits he still collects tactics "quite often right now."

But when I looked back at my life, I didn't really have anything to show for it." He couldn't articulate what he remembered, his life didn't change much, and he felt he was falling behind. # SECCIÓN 14.4 — Build to learn


## CLÚSTER C-T07a-13: Build to learn (learn as you build)
- idea: Es la idea central del bloque: "don't learn to build, build to learn"; "if you aren't building you aren't learning". Aprender sin aplicarlo a un problema que está en tu mente es acumular conocimiento inútil, "soon to be forgotten brain fog"; leer sin proyecto es ocio. Lo primero al empezar a aprender no es estudiar sino crear un proyecto (una cuenta de Twitter para aprender copywriting) que dé el marco para filtrar la señal: sin proyecto ni meta, toda la información es ruido. "You don't learn skills, you build projects": las habilidades, como los títulos, son casi inútiles aisladas; el proyecto es una "quest" que permite la interest-based education y puede ser tu propio cuerpo. Tratar todo como proyecto implica bosquejar (outline) e investigar intencionalmente para llenarlo. Límite: leer un libro de negocios sin negocio es "kind of dumb", pero la filosofía funciona siempre porque tu vida es el proyecto. Learning, building y distributing son los hábitos de una vida mejor, porque el yo desarrollado debe intercambiarse con otros.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: formulación estable desde "become a builder" (2022) hasta "expansion pack" (2026); la versión de 2024-07 introduce el límite explícito entre dominios técnicos (requieren proyecto) y dominios de big picture como la filosofía (la vida es el proyecto).
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

## CLÚSTER C-T07a-17: Forced synchronicity and the law of inspired action (why building works)
- idea: Dos razones por las que construir hace aprender (2022). Forced synchronicity: novedad, reconocimiento de patrones y dopamina son cruciales para aprender, y el conocimiento se retiene cuando se alinea con la supervivencia; un proyecto significativo es algo que quieres que "sobreviva", así que notas lo que puede hacerlo crecer. La metáfora del ancestro y el arbusto lo ilustra: recuerda las bayas nuevas porque sirven para sobrevivir, no una hoja nueva; hoy ese mecanismo se gasta en sobrevivir al concepto de uno mismo, y el proyecto lo redirige. Law of inspired action: con un proyecto uno se ve atraído a consumir lo que lo inspira y alimenta, idealmente justo antes de trabajar (Faulkner: la inspiración "strikes at nine every morning"); pero "you have to start building the damn business in order to apply those teachings". Combinando construir y enseñar se puede convertir a uno mismo en el proyecto: tratar toda la vida como algo que se construye, poda y reconstruye produce novedad, sincronicidad y serendipia.
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-020-012
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** Forced synchronicity: a project makes novelty, pattern recognition and dopamine work for learning
- **desarrollo:** First reason building works. "Novelty pattern recognition and dopamine are crucial for learning": when you make a discovery — noticing patterns, or how one thing can be used in another area — the dopamine and excitement make your brain ready to understand it. Knowledge is retained when it aligns with survival. A meaningful project is something you want to "survive" — to progress, develop and live — so with it in your awareness you notice things that align with it and can be applied to grow it. That is "forced synchronicity."
- **ejemplos:** The ancestor and the berry bush (U-020-013).
- **cita:** "when you have a project that is Meaningful you will notice things in order to survive that project"
- **terminos:** forced synchronicity, novelty, pattern recognition, dopamine
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-020-010
- **fuente:** Learn New Skills FAST (How I Remember Everything I Learn).md, 9:01–10:39, 2022-11-27
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-020-013
- **tipo:** metáfora
- **titulo:** The ancestor and the bush: novelty is remembered only when it has something to be applied to
- **desarrollo:** An ancestor walking past the same bush over and over gets conditioned to see it doesn't aid survival — no fruit, no berries, "it was just a bush." When they notice something new, like berries growing, it catches their attention and they are much more likely to remember it next time if they need it for survival. But if the novelty were just a new leaf, with nothing to apply it to, they wouldn't remember it. The ancestor used berries for physical survival; in the modern age, much of what we do with information and ideas is trying to "survive the notion or the concept of self" — the idea of what we are — which causes pain and suffering. A meaningful project redirects this survival-noticing mechanism toward something productive.
- **ejemplos:** Berries vs. new leaf on the bush.
- **cita:** "a lot of what we're trying to do in this Modern Age with information and ideas is we're trying to survive the notion or the concept of self"
- **terminos:** survive the concept of self
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-020-012
- **fuente:** Learn New Skills FAST (How I Remember Everything I Learn).md, 9:01–10:39, 2022-11-27
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-020-014
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** The law of inspired action: a project entices you to consume what inspires and fuels it, ideally right before working
- **desarrollo:** Second reason building works. He quotes William Faulkner: "I only write when inspiration strikes fortunately it strikes at nine every morning." Everyone acts better when inspired: before training chest at the gym you might watch a chest motivation or workout video so form and motivation are top of mind. Before writing, he reads authors, ideas and writing that inspire him and give him ideas. With a project, "you are enticed to learn in accordance with it because it will inspire you to keep that project going." If you consume something that helps your project before working on it, or build that into your routine (he points to his "fill empty use framework" video), it keeps the momentum of the project's growth. If you are 100% committed to a business, you will naturally gravitate to consuming information that helps build it — with the preface that you are not distracted and actively eliminate distractions — "but you have to start building the damn business in order to apply those teachings."
- **ejemplos:** Chest motivation video before the gym; reading inspiring authors before writing; commitment to building a business.
- **cita:** "you have to start build building the damn business in order to apply those teachings"
- **terminos:** law of inspired action, fill empty use framework
- **origen:** adaptada-de:William Faulkner
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-020-010
- **fuente:** Learn New Skills FAST (How I Remember Everything I Learn).md, 10:39–12:17, 2022-11-27
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-020-015
- **tipo:** fuente-de-tercero
- **titulo:** William Faulkner's quote on inspiration striking at nine every morning
- **desarrollo:** The author cites Faulkner — "I only write when inspiration strikes fortunately it strikes at nine every morning" — to support the idea that inspiration can be scheduled and engineered: by consuming inspiring material before working on a project (as part of a routine), you put yourself into the inspired state on demand.
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "I only write when inspiration strikes fortunately it strikes at nine every morning"
- **terminos:** ninguno
- **origen:** de-tercero:William Faulkner
- **nivel:** complementario

- **tension:** ninguna


### U-020-019
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Building helps retain knowledge, teaching retains even more; combine both by making yourself the project
- **desarrollo:** Chain: building helps you retain a lot → teaching is another modality that solidifies knowledge further → so combine the two "to create a life of novelty synchronicity and Serendipity," increasing both knowledge retained and enjoyment of life, by creating a lifestyle conducive to learning what you want to learn. How: "by turning you into the project." Building a project doesn't only apply to external things like a Photoshop project; if you treat your entire life as a project "that you build and grow and cut down and rebuild and just have fun with along the way," everything you learn can be applied to your life.
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "if you treat your entire life as a project that you build and grow and cut down and rebuild and just have fun with along the way then life becomes that much better"
- **terminos:** turning you into the project, novelty, synchronicity, serendipity
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-020-010
- **fuente:** Learn New Skills FAST (How I Remember Everything I Learn).md, 13:52–15:33, 2022-11-27
- **tension:** ninguna



## CLÚSTER C-T07b-06: The philosophy of the Builder ("learning comes from struggle, not memorization")
- idea: Aprender no significa nada sin construir. La vida del autor fue un "Meta project" hecho de proyectos personales y de negocio; los proyectos enmarcan la atención y filtran toda la información. La fuente del aprendizaje es la lucha: encontrar un problema, descubrir su solución e integrarla; para identificar un problema necesitas una meta, y para resolverlo, un proyecto de 1–6 meses. "Reading books is not learning, watching tutorials is not learning; you learn by building a project and facing reality." Un proyecto tiene outline, milestones (jerarquía de metas), un deadline real y experimentación. El ciclo: crear proyecto → camino/sistema → construir con lo que sabes → enseñar → detectar knowledge gaps → estudiar conocimiento profundo → repetir. La realidad enseña miles de matices que ningún curso puede mostrar; ninguna idea es ley sin el filtro de la experiencia directa ("even if that advice is my own"); la teoría lleva al nivel intermedio y la experiencia a la intuición del experto. Fuentes de apoyo: Aristóteles ("the purpose of knowledge is action") y Schmachtenberger ("learning is the process of life").
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: la fuerza de la negación de la lectura varía: "reading books is not learning" (2023-12, U-020-075) frente a otros videos donde la lectura intencional es una entrada clave (C-T07b-29/31). Ver Evolución.
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-017-199
- **tipo:** proceso
- **titulo:** Project-based learning loop: create project → create path/system → build with what you know → teach → identify knowledge gaps → study deep knowledge → repeat forever.
- **desarrollo:** "To learn information with efficiency, don't start learning first." Steps: (1) create a project for the goal you want to achieve; (2) create a path or system to achieve that goal; (3) start building the project in the real world with what you know; (4) teach people about what you are building (friends, family or internet peers); (5) identify knowledge gaps when you can't build further or articulate your thoughts; (6) study deep knowledge to fill those gaps; (7) return to building and teaching; repeat forever. This process uses novelty, pattern recognition and self-experimentation to maximize the flow of information you experience. Book quote: "Most people learn and learn and learn just to start over when it comes time to build because they realize that everything they've learned has no direct use in the project they began; they don't have a goal-specific lens to interpret their learnings from."

## CLÚSTER C-T07b-11: Start with the purpose (the guitar-song method)
- idea: "If I want to learn how to play the guitar, I don't start learning, I start playing", porque el propósito de la guitarra es tocar una canción. Primero el propósito; ante el dilema (no sabes tocarla) aprendes solo la técnica necesaria para el siguiente paso, en ciclos de trial and error; la primera canción sale en unas dos semanas, la siguiente en una, luego en tres días, luego una por día, y para entonces tienes 80–90% de los fundamentos. Lo mismo para software creativo: en vez del tour de una hora por todas las herramientas de Photoshop o After Effects, define la imagen que quieres crear, busca el tutorial más parecido a tu proyecto, filtra lo inútil y apila técnicas ("select and mask" cubre ~50% de Photoshop). Resumen del autor: "it's literally just do things".
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-020-170
- **tipo:** proceso
- **titulo:** Start with the purpose, not learning: to learn guitar, start playing a song; learn only the technique for the next step
- **desarrollo:** "If I want to learn how to play the guitar, I don't start learning, I start playing. Because the entire purpose of playing the guitar is to play a song." Step one: start with the purpose — what are you trying to achieve? Then the dilemma: you have a song in mind but can't play it — "that's when you start learning in the traditional sense, but now your learning is focused": not an entire textbook, just the little technique needed for the next step. Can't read the first note? Look up a cheat sheet for sheet music; then how to play it on guitar — a certain finger placement — "sounds decent." Repeat trial (experimenting) and error (correcting or learning the specific knowledge for your unique goal) and you'll probably play the song, even if terribly, likely within two weeks.
- **ejemplos:** Sheet-music cheat sheet; finger placement.
- **cita:** "I don't start learning, I start playing."
- **terminos:** start with the purpose, trial and error
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental

- **desarrollo:** For the next song you already have a group of techniques, making it easier. "Let's say you learn the next song in a week and then the next one in 3 days and then you can learn one song a day. By that point, you have most of what it takes" — "80 to 90% of the fundamentals of playing the guitar." Then it's showing up daily and slowly increasing the challenge over 1, 2, 3 years.

### U-020-173
- **tipo:** ejemplo
- **titulo:** Creatives and Photoshop: skip the hour-long tool tour; to put a tree on a new background, learn select-and-mask (~50% of Photoshop)
- **desarrollo:** For creatives (most of his audience), Photoshop, After Effects, Premiere "increase your ability to take your mind and put it into reality." Most watch an hour-long tutorial on "every single tool that lives in the Photoshop sidebar" — 95% of which you won't apply. Instead start with what you're trying to do: create an image, e.g., put a tree on a different background. Narrow learning to removing a tree's background; watching a few videos shows many ways; experiment and find that a specific select-and-mask technique is better for a tree (more complex) than a plain object. Once done, it solidifies: "I know this technique exists. I can do it that much faster the next time." "Selecting and masking objects gets you like 50% of the way through Photoshop." As techniques stack you save countless hours "learning nonsense" and can create most things.
- **ejemplos:** Tree background removal with select and mask.
- **cita:** "selecting and masking objects gets you like 50% of the way through Photoshop"
- **terminos:** select and mask, stacking techniques

- **nivel:** intermedio

Instead "you need something to actually build ... a vision or an image of it in your head." "You need to start the project with the knowledge you have and if you don't have knowledge, then you find a specific tutorial that is as similar to the project you want to create as it can be." E.g., for a minimalist line animation (as he used to post on Instagram), find a tutorial of that or something close "so that I can directly apply what I'm learning in the moment." "Most of the tutorial will be useless, but you'll be filtering it out"; repeat "until you stack enough techniques" — not every tool in the sidebar — "to get the result that you want."

## CLÚSTER C-T12a-19: The Build Teach Earn method
- idea: Método para principiantes absolutos que "bakes in every single thing you need to earn a living as a creator". (1) Build: autoeducarte mientras construyes algo tangible; como todos los negocios son hoy "content companies, media companies", cada habilidad del negocio creador (diseño, copywriting, redes, marketing, publicidad) se puede vender a otras empresas. (2) Teach: enseñar revela los huecos de tu conocimiento ("I'm not good enough, and you'd be correct, you aren't; neither is anyone"). (3) Earn: con 5.000–10.000 seguidores estás calificado para ayudar a empresas, y tu nicho es un problema concreto resuelto de forma única. (3.5) Vender lo que tú comprarías. (4) Earn more: productizar resultados y apalancar la audiencia. Los servicios de negocio y marketing son aptos para principiantes porque igual necesitarás esas habilidades para vender lo que de verdad quieras.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-009-209

- **titulo:** The Build Teach Earn method: build to learn, teach to enhance learning and build an audience, earn by helping, earn more by leveraging results
- **desarrollo:** For absolute beginners, "the build teach earn method." Dan had several skills and a freelancing business before becoming a creator; the method helped him succeed much faster and "bakes in every single thing you need to earn a living as a creator." Steps: (1) Build — to learn high-value skills to earn with; (2) Teach — to enhance your learning and build an audience; (3) Earn — by helping others with what you've learned; (3.5) an equally viable route: create a product or service you would buy, use and benefit from (sell what's already selling); (4) Earn more — by leveraging your results and an audience (productize your results). Each step detailed in following units.

### U-009-210

- **desarrollo:** Step one: build to learn high-value skills. "The best way to learn is to self-educate so you have knowledge as you build something tangible so you have experience." Few creators realize most businesses are the same nowadays: "they're all content companies, they're all media companies," acquiring customers online with brand, content, product and promotions. So the skills that make your creator business succeed can be offered to other businesses as a freelance service or digital product. A creator business is running an entire business as one person; you need every skill that makes a business work. Learn graphic design as you build your profile picture, banner, website images, thumbnails; copywriting and content writing as you build landing pages, website, social content and newsletters; social media ("yes it's a skill") to build following, network and authority; marketing and sales as you build your product or service; advertising and promotions as you promote to get buyers.

Rather than seeing it as the problem of not knowing enough, see it as an opportunity to learn and fill the holes: "that's what teaching does is it reveals the gaps in your knowledge." People write it off when "it is literally the only opportunity to learn and solidify that learning staring you in the face." Content: teach your skill set, your mindset, make sense of complex ideas from your point of view, teach your polarizing beliefs ("everyone should be a carnivore and why").

# SECCIÓN 14.5 — Enseñar es aprender; aprender en público


## CLÚSTER C-T07a-19: Build as you learn in public (your own brand as practice ground)
- idea: Las redes sociales son "the perfect vessel to build as you learn": para aprender diseño gráfico, diseña tu foto de perfil, banner y web con tutoriales específicos. Se aprende más cuando se crea y se tiene dónde publicarlo (accountability y feedback directo), sin necesidad de convertirlo en negocio. Practicar en el propio negocio da resultados reales (landing page para un producto de afiliado antes de cobrar 1.000–2.000 dólares a otros), a diferencia de portafolios que nunca se cobran; también vale la "permissionless apprenticeship" (hacer la web de tu marca favorita). La marca personal es el mejor campo de práctica porque reúne muchas habilidades de alto valor. Learning with intention: 30–50 horas (o 50–100) de contenido gratuito y cursos, construyendo la marca en paralelo; mientras tanto se puede ganar dinero desde ya y subir precios por etapas hasta productizar.
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: ninguna (las cifras de horas varían: 30–50 en 2023, 50–100 en 2024).
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-009-099

## CLÚSTER C-T07a-20: Learn < build < teach (protégé effect and Feynman technique)
- idea: Aprender solo enseña algo; construir enseña más (hay a qué aplicar y problemas que exigen specific knowledge); enseñar lo solidifica aún más porque obliga a articular la comprensión y revela los knowledge gaps (protégé effect: "the teacher learns more than the student"). Al principio se enseña mal (sus primeros videos), se detecta qué falló y se vuelve a enseñar desde un lugar mejor. La técnica de Feynman formaliza el ciclo: elegir un concepto, explicarlo en lenguaje simple como a un niño, identificar los huecos, volver a la fuente y simplificar. La versión de Sahil Bloom: enseñar lo antes posible a un amigo sin contexto, observar dónde se confunde o se interesa, estudiar los huecos y repetir (teach-learn loop). Formas de enseñar: escribir en público y aceptar la crítica, enseñar a amigos, tomar notas como si te enseñaras a ti mismo; escribir es la mejor vía y es más potente en público, con feedback y ligado al trabajo. Enseñar aumenta además el reconocimiento de patrones: la vida se vuelve serendípica.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: las cifras de retención de 2022 ("some study", 90% vs 5%) desaparecen en 2024–2025, donde el protégé effect se define formalmente y se añade el aumento del reconocimiento de patrones. Tensión con C-T07a-38/40: en 2022 las notas retienen "5%" frente a enseñar, mientras que después el autor construye sistemas y software de notas (allí las notas se presentan como materia prima para crear y enseñar).
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-020-032
- **tipo:** framework
- **titulo:** Step 5 — Teach what you know: learn < build < teach (graphic of nested circles)
- **desarrollo:** He shows a simple graphic (circles whose white outline represents how much you learn doing each thing — exact visual not reconstructible from the transcript). Starting/learning alone teaches some; building teaches more because you have something to apply learning to, encounter problems and seek specific knowledge; teaching "solidifies it that much better" because you're forced to articulate your understanding, and you can identify knowledge gaps in what you're trying to teach. It will suck at first: his first YouTube video and all his early teaching sucked, but he identified what sucked, filled in the blanks, came back and retaught it "from a better place." This is known as the Protégé effect: learning by teaching.
- **ejemplos:** His first YouTube videos.
- **cita:** "you're forced to articulate your understanding of it"
- **terminos:** Protégé effect, learning by teaching, knowledge gaps
- **origen:** adaptada-de:Protégé effect (psychology)
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-020-019
- **fuente:** Learn New Skills FAST (How I Remember Everything I Learn).md, 24:32–25:21, 2022-11-27
- **tension:** Ambiguity: the graphic is described only partially.

When you teach "you pay closer attention when you learn and identify knowledge gaps that help you learn faster."

- **desarrollo:** The Feynman technique "overlaps with What's called the Protege effect which in short is the teacher learns more than the student." "Teaching what you know or what you learn encourages you to make sense of it in your own way to finally string it together in your own mind ... that's learning." It "increases the effect of pattern recognition life becomes more enjoyable and serendipitous because you notice more things in life that can help you achieve the projects or goals ... you learn more just from living."

### U-005-103
- **tipo:** fuente-de-tercero
- **titulo:** Sahil's version of the Feynman Technique: teach as fast as possible, watch where the listener gets confused, study the gaps, repeat (teach-learn loop).
- **desarrollo:** Sahil is "a big believer in the Feynman Technique," derived from Richard Feynman ("American theoretical physicist, won the Nobel Prize in quantum electrodynamics"), known for "his ability to convey very complex topics in simple terms" to the uninitiated. Principle: "teaching is the most powerful form of learning... you cannot teach something effectively if you don't deeply understand it." His method: (1) "I default to teaching as quickly as possible" — after reading something or a conversation, "try to explain it to another friend who didn't have any context on it"; (2) observe their reaction: "where do I have gaps in my own understanding... where did I see their interest piqued or where did I see them get confused?"; (3) "go study to fill in those gaps"; (4) "come back and try to do it again." This creates "this iterative loop of teach, learn, teach, learn that leads to this depth of knowledge very, very quickly." Contrast: if you "go study in a whole and then... try to teach," you may have accumulated "knowledge that is not useful for actually creating actionable insight."
- **ejemplos:** Explaining a concept to a friend with no context.
- **cita:** "this iterative loop of teach, learn, teach, learn that leads to this depth of knowledge very, very quickly"
- **terminos:** Feynman Technique; teach-learn loop
- **origen:** adaptada-de:Richard Feynman, via Sahil Bloom
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** ninguno
- **fuente:** The Time Billionaire (A Concept That Will Change Your Life) With Sahil Bloom.md, 44:41-46:20, 2025-01-30
- **tension:** ninguna

- **desarrollo:** "A learning method popularized by physicist Richard Feynman in short it's about deeply understanding a concept by explaining it in simple terms as if you were teaching it to someone with no prior knowledge." Steps: (1) choose a concept — select the topic; (2) teach it — explain in simple language "as if you were teaching it to a child"; (3) identify gaps — where you struggle to explain clearly, your understanding is weak; (4) review and simplify — go back to the source material, relearn, and explain again in even simpler terms.
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "explaining it in simple terms as if you were teaching it to someone with no prior knowledge"
- **terminos:** Feynman technique
- **origen:** de-tercero:Richard Feynman
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-021-130
- **fuente:** How To Learn Anything 10x Faster Than Anyone With AI.md, 24:57–25:46, 2025-03-09
- **tension:** ninguna

## CLÚSTER C-T07b-08: Personal brand as a learning ground (permissionless apprenticeship)
- idea: Un proyecto es una meta estructurada que revela el skill stack que necesitas: "you need the goal or project first and then they all reveal themselves". La marca personal es el campo de práctica ideal porque te obliga a aprender diseño (banner, foto de perfil), copywriting (bio), escritura de contenido, newsletter como embudo y productos; te da "a year to learn these and somewhere to apply them", fuera del tutorial hell. Alternativa: la "permissionless apprenticeship" —crear algo para una marca o creador (un sitio, una secuencia de emails, una landing page) y enviarlo o publicarlo etiquetándolo, sin necesidad ni venta—, que a la vez enseña y atrae oportunidades.
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-002-027
- **tipo:** principio
- **titulo:** Start, then learn: a project is a structured goal that reveals the skill stack you need
- **desarrollo:** Dan connects Dickie's story to a YouTube video of his (retitled about five times, something like "learn new skills fast"): "start then learn" — you have to actually build something. It is becoming "more and more of a pattern of like Behavior change where you're trying to survive a project": a project is "a structured goal in a sense where it has other things that bring Clarity to it." This is why he thinks everyone interested should have a personal brand — even for career opportunities, since "employers are hiring from the value that you provide." Building his brand introduced him to the whole skill stack: graphic design for the banner and profile picture, visual composition, what creates a compelling bio ("which kind of revolves around top of funnel persuasion"), writing content short/medium/long, the distinction between content and marketing (sales page, email, "how does that all flow"), designing an idea for Instagram, building a product, monetizing. People ask "what skills do I learn" — "you need the goal or project first and then they all reveal themselves."
- **ejemplos:** The personal-brand skill stack listed above.
- **cita:** "you need the goal or project first and then they all reveal themselves"

## CLÚSTER C-T07b-09: Teaching is learning (the protégé effect)
- idea: "Everyone's afraid to teach when teaching is how you learn." Por el protégé effect, quien enseña (o se prepara para enseñar) aprende más que el alumno: el esfuerzo cognitivo de explicar obliga a comprender, organizar y detectar knowledge gaps. Escribir es enseñar; enseñar en público funciona como "public resume" y atrae oportunidades; el autor dice aprender "10 times faster" porque enseña (incluso a su novia por la noche). Online hay dos opciones: consumer (se vuelve "mentally overweight") o contributor. No hace falta saberlo antes: se puede compartir con honestidad lo que se aprendió hoy; los creadores articulados solo lo parecen porque no viste sus malas iteraciones.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna (la explicación del mecanismo se vuelve más técnica en 2026).
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

You don't have to lie: be honest, "hey I learned this today how interesting is this," or give your honest experience in a bullet point list (e.g. why you should start a one-person business). "No one's stopping you but yourself from writing and producing some form of value in the world."
- **ejemplos:** "I learned this today"; a bullet list of why to start a one-person business.
- **cita:** "everyone's afraid to teach when teaching is how you learn"
- **terminos:** ninguno

### U-006-118
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** Writing as teaching and the protégé effect: the teacher learns more than the student, and articulate creators only seem so because you never saw their bad iterations.
- **desarrollo:** Dan Koe: "think of your writing as teaching because that's what it is," and leverage "the protégé effect which is that... the teacher learns more than the student where teaching helps you learn more faster." Analogy: after reading a book you excitedly explain it to a friend; "you don't articulate it very well the first time around right but then over time that's what helps it make sense to you." "People that you watch online aren't articulate because they're just magically articulate it's because they've practiced and you don't notice the bad iterations because people don't care about you when you are first starting out when you have zero followers zero experience people don't care until you've actually gained the experience by doing it over and over again."
- **ejemplos:** Explaining a book to a friend.
- **cita:** "you don't notice the bad iterations because people don't care about you when you are first starting out"
- **terminos:** protégé effect
- **origen:** propia (adaptada-de:the protégé effect, no author named)
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-006-117
- **fuente:** The Future Of Work (Avoid Learning These Skills).md, 36:10–36:54, 2024-07-28
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-023-195
- **tipo:** fuente-de-tercero
- **titulo:** Step three: teach what you learn — the protégé effect; teach in public as your modern resume
- **desarrollo:** "The protégé effect: a psychological phenomenon where teaching, pretending to teach or preparing to teach information to others helps a person learn that information". When you teach you pay closer attention when learning and identify knowledge gaps that help you learn faster. He recommends teaching in public as a personal brand, "your public resume or the modern resume" (your social profile), attracting high-paying opportunities as you learn. You don't need to know it before writing: "you write, so you teach, and you understand it further". "I learn things 10 times faster because I teach them". Doing it online makes it part of your life and you evolve faster; it's "not really a career, it's the collapse of work and life into one".
- **ejemplos:** Explaining animation, art or a difficult book concept to his girlfriend at night: identifies what he doesn't know, creating a new problem that frames perception, so he picks up the information that fills the gap much faster.
- **cita:** "I learn things 10 times faster because I teach them to other people"
- **terminos:** protégé effect; public resume; modern resume; collapse of work and life into one
- **origen:** adaptada-de:efecto protegido (psicología)
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-023-192
- **fuente:** Life Is A Video Game (Here's How You Win).md, 52:14-53:45, 2024-07-21
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-027-238
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** Document your life: the protégé effect—we learn better by teaching; consumer vs. contributor
- **desarrollo:** The protégé effect: "a concept in learning that suggests we learn more effectively by teaching information to others even if those others aren't necessarily any less knowledgeable." Koe has noticed it personally. There are two options online: consumer or contributor (he prefers "contributor" to "content creator"). The consumer takes, gets "mentally overweight", never pieces things together or shares a perspective. Sharing returns tenfold: you actually learn, get direct feedback on beliefs, and are encouraged to challenge and reshape thoughts. "The teacher learns more than the student": he learns far more writing and making videos—synthesizing his own worldview and sharing it—than by consuming, forced to solve his own problems and help others, approaching problems from a different perspective. When you solve problems, think how to pass it down uniquely.
- **ejemplos:** Koe's writing and videos.
- **cita:** "there's two options there's consumer or contributor"
- **terminos:** Protege effect; consumer; contributor; mentally overweight
- **origen:** adaptada-de:protégé effect (concepto de aprendizaje)
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-027-234
- **fuente:** The Fastest Way To Ruin Your Life (Avoid This While You're Young).md, 29:02–31:24, 2025-01-12
- **tension:** ninguna

I don't know if you should teach everything, but you can share what you're learning to see if you can understand it and you put it in writing or video or whatever it may be."

### U-014-185
- **tipo:** fuente-de-tercero
- **titulo:** The protégé effect: teaching material produces better retention than merely studying it
- **desarrollo:** In learning science, "the protégé effect" shows that students who teach material retain it better than students who merely study it. Mechanism as the author gives it: the cognitive load required to figure out how to explain something to someone else forces deeper understanding, better organization, and identification of gaps in knowledge. Writing, as a form of teaching, harnesses it.

## CLÚSTER C-T10a-18: Build, learn and document in public
- idea: Construir debe hacerse en público porque necesitas datos del mercado para saber si algo es valioso: el starving artist se apega a su versión del valor. La pieza faltante para hacer de tu obsesión un trabajo de tiempo completo es "people who care about what you're building", y la mejor forma de mostrarlo es escribir y enseñar lo que aprendes mientras construyes (Pieter Levels construyendo un juego con IA en público). Si no te gusta la idea de ser content creator, piensa en las redes como mecanismo de trabajo independiente: tomar notas en público, replantear el aprendizaje como investigación. No necesitas experiencia si entiendes perspectiva y positioning y das crédito a tus fuentes. Mejorarte en público fija lo aprendido y, con el tiempo, da audiencia y producto. Documentar crea relaciones durante años (los youtubers de fitness que marcaron a Dan) y la "public forcing function" de Dickie Bush (30 hilos en 30 días, el hilo sobre Balaji retuiteado por Naval, el producto de $20) muestra cómo convertirse en "trusted curator".
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna (formulación estable 2022–2026; en 2026 se dirige explícitamente a quien no quiere identificarse como content creator).
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-007-176
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Build in public because you need data: starving artists get attached to their version of value
- **desarrollo:** Building must be done in public: posting, building, publishing, launching, because you need data to know if information is valuable. "Starving artists, it's in the name": they create what they think is valuable, get attached to their version of value, and wonder why nothing sells ("this painting is beautiful"). Fine as a hobby, but full-time you play "a back and forth game with the market". Since you're trying to improve people's lives, if people don't buy, read or stay attentive, is it really valuable? If it doesn't get results, iterate and pivot.
- **ejemplos:** starving artist's painting
- **cita:** "you're going to have to play a back and forth game with the market"
- **terminos:** build in public; starving artists; data
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** none
- **fuente:** You Have A $100,000 Product In Your Head (One Person Business Series).md, 20:49-22:25, 2022-12-04
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-021-129
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** The missing puzzle piece: people who care about what you're building — shown through writing in public
- **desarrollo:** The puzzle: you know what you want, you have projects that take you there, you learn and build daily — "there's one missing piece and it turns out it's also a way to further enhance how much you learn": "people who care about what You're Building." Assumption: you want to do this full-time, "replace your job or career with a calling an obsession," which needs money — "at least enough to sustain your ideal life you don't need millions." To make people care "you need to show what you're doing in public and in most cases the best way to do that is writing teaching what you know and what you're learning as you build." Master class: Pieter Levels (levels.io) building a game with AI in public, adding features followers wanted — attracting people who care and an audience that wants to support you.
- **ejemplos:** Pieter Levels building a game with AI in public.
- **cita:** "the missing piece is people who care about what You're Building"
- **terminos:** calling, obsession, build in public
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental

- **tension:** ninguna

- **desarrollo:** "If you don't like the idea of becoming a content creator, just think of social media as a mechanism to do independent work." "If you love learning, great. Reframe it as research and now that's literally your main job. Most of the things I write about simply come from me learning about my interests and treating social media like I'm taking notes in public. So, you're already spending time learning. Now, just spend that time learning in public and boom, you have the foundation of a business."

### U-011-025
- **tipo:** principio
- **titulo:** You don't need experience to post online; learn in public with perspective and positioning
- **desarrollo:** You don't need experience to post a video, article, or anything online, especially if you understand perspective and "positioning, which is a marketing term." To make it impactful and effective you need to understand marketing, psychology, persuasion, possibly epistemology and philosophy — "how we make sense of things": stories, metaphors, concepts, what catches attention, what holds attention, what is deemed valuable. You can "learn it in public": post your perspective on what you are learning and give credit to people. There are ways around not having experience and building experience while posting videos, articles, tweets.
- **ejemplos:** Posting your perspective on something you're learning while giving credit to sources.

- **desarrollo:** The best way to retain what you learn about the things you love: (1) improve yourself in public with a personal brand; (2) learn the skills necessary to build it, so you remember those skills; (3) teach those skills or your interests, retaining that knowledge. A personal brand is "Dynamic": not limited to one niche because it's under your name, so you can dive into many domains "as long as you're making your interest interesting to other people." Over time you build an audience, then create a product to monetize — "that's how you make a living for yourself" if you stick it out long enough.

### U-002-002
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Creators build relationships with their audience simply by documenting their journey over years
- **desarrollo:** Dickie observes that Dan developed a relationship with the fitness YouTubers he watched (videos seven or eight years old) just by them documenting, to the point that they influenced Dan's entire journey even though Dan is no longer in the fitness space. Both say "me too." The documented journey of someone you look up to shapes your path beyond the original topic. Later in the episode this becomes the rationale for doing recurring conversations: people can watch the series and see "these guys that I look up to didn't just happen here." Following one person's or one small community's journey over time is presented as better than a podcast that branches into too many domains.
- **ejemplos:** Matt Ogus, Chris Lovato; the plan for a yearly rotation of ~4 people (Dickie, Joey, Justin, Scott); prior episodes on each other's channels (October 2021 and ~June 2022).
- **cita:** "you have developed a relationship with those people just by them documenting"
- **terminos:** documenting; shared goal, shared perspective
- **origen:** de-tercero:Dickie Bush (observation) / propia (series idea)
- **nivel:** complementario
- **prerrequisitos:** ninguno
- **fuente:** Dan Koe & Dickie Bush On One-Person Businesses, Creative Workflows, and Lifestyle Design.md, [6:28], [3:16:40]–[3:17:32], 2023-03-16
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-002-025
- **tipo:** caso
- **titulo:** Dickie's first internet dollars: 30 days of podcast-note Twitter threads, the Balaji thread, and a $20 product
- **desarrollo:** Dickie began writing in January 2020 while at BlackRock: a weekly newsletter plus weekly blog post for 40 weeks that "basically no one was reading" except his mom. Feeling stuck, ready to give up and stay in finance, he took "one last shot": write on Twitter instead of the blog, and instead of framing himself as an expert (e.g., productivity), take something he already did — listening to podcasts on daily walks and taking notes that lived "in the back of a notion folder" he never looked at — and tweet a Twitter thread distilling one podcast every day for 30 days. Why it worked: (a) "the public forcing function of putting those ideas in a concise way" made him internalize them; (b) the social environment (vs. a blog) let people interact, attracting people with the same interests; (c) people began to see him "as a trusted curator and source of information." On day 28 (June/July 2020) he distilled Balaji Srinivasan's worldview from ~10 podcasts; he woke to Naval having retweeted it, 4–5k likes, and his newsletter went from ~100 subscribers in 40 weeks to 1,000 in 24 hours. People then asked if the ideas were compiled anywhere; he said no but he could, packaged it that weekend, put it for sale Monday on Gumroad for $20 and made $500. Learning how to launch and fulfill it spiraled into Ship 30.
- **ejemplos:** Balaji thread retweeted by Naval; $20 Gumroad product, $500.
- **cita:** "I didn't have the public forcing function of putting those ideas in a concise way I wasn't really internalizing them"
- **terminos:** public forcing function; trusted curator; 30-day challenge
- **origen:** de-tercero:Dickie Bush
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** ninguno
- **fuente:** Dan Koe & Dickie Bush On One-Person Businesses, Creative Workflows, and Lifestyle Design.md, [48:47]–[53:50], 2023-03-16
- **tension:** ninguna



## CLÚSTER C-T10b-24: Learn and teach in public (social media as your public journal)
- idea: No se trata de "convertirse en content creator", sino de usar las redes como un diario público en vez de un lugar para distraerse: escribir lo que aprendes duplica el aprendizaje (forcing function, efecto protegido), atrae una audiencia de intereses similares que será tu red y tus futuros clientes y construye leverage frente al reemplazo laboral. Escribir en público y dejar que te critiquen es lo que te muestra los errores; incluso la gente callada se vuelve conversadora al enseñar. "A personal brand is just discovering yourself in public and getting paid for the research": cuando tratas tu vida como un proyecto, se vuelve rentable al convertirla en producto. Publicar no exige mentalidad emprendedora: aprendes más rápido, consigues oportunidades y ves tus puntos ciegos.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-021-133

- **titulo:** Treat social media as your public journal, not as becoming a content creator
- **desarrollo:** To merge the Feynman technique and protégé effect with what you're learning, "you do it by writing on the internet." "I'm not talking about becoming a content creator I'm talking about treating social media as your public Journal rather than a place to just distract yourself." That gives you a chance at attracting "potential supporters customers employers investors team members." Learning, building and writing to systematically reflect in public "doubles down on the effect of learning." "It's less about building following and more about putting your work in front of other people" — like meeting new people: it won't happen if you don't leave your house, and you'll grow bitter wondering why you're alone; "your chances of success are zero."
- **ejemplos:** Not leaving the house and wondering why you're alone.
- **cita:** "treating social media as your public Journal rather than a place to just distract yourself"

- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental

- **desarrollo:** Writing on the internet or social media is "the new media" — how you build any audience or distribution for independent creative work. Teaching in public opens: (1) "you learn much much faster because you have a forcing function for understanding"; (2) "you attract an audience of people with similar interests and these people are your network and future customers"; (3) "you build leverage going into a future of job replacement. The creator economy is one area that is proving to not be replaced even though everyone screams there will be an overabundance of content."

- **desarrollo:** "Write in public and let people criticize you, because without the criticism you're not going to learn how to fix what you had wrong". Teach friends via interesting conversation; if you're naturally quiet, learn something and say "hey I was learning about this new thing, it's pretty cool"; now you're conversational, because you must structure what you say, explain it, ensure you aren't giving false knowledge, and you know what to learn next. He doesn't know everything when writing newsletters or videos, but writing four years on topics he's interested in has accelerated his knowledge and wisdom far more than not creating. "The modern way to create is content and products": life becomes a meaningful series of solving problems, learning to solve them, teaching others, and earning as you go — "what evolution has allowed", doing what you love without barriers thanks to the internet.

## CLÚSTER C-T12b-19: Personal brand as the practice ground for every skill
- idea: La forma favorita del autor de integrar y practicar todas las skills es empezar un one-person business o personal brand "porque es un negocio": SEO en tu propio blog, diseño con tu banner, persuasión con tu contenido, copy para tu propia página o un minimum viable offer, o un producto de afiliado. Practicar en tu propio negocio da resultados reales en vez de portfolio pieces, y luego puedes vender esas habilidades (landing pages por $1.000–2.000) cobrando "10 times more" que quien solo aprendió diseño web y manda cold emails sin experiencia. El skill stack de un año construyendo marca se transfiere a casi cualquier negocio online. Como paso 2 de un proceso de aprendizaje: delinear un proyecto personal con potencial rentable (preferentemente en Twitter, que solo exige teclear), porque "if you're just learning and not doing anything with it you're not learning".
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-013-065
- **tipo:** método
- **titulo:** A personal brand / one-person business as the practice ground for every skill
- **desarrollo:** His favorite way to integrate and practice all skills immediately is starting a one-person business, a personal brand, "because it's a business." Freelancers think "I want to practice SEO so I'll try to land an SEO client," but you could start your own website about what you're building and doing in life and practice SEO on your own blog; practice design with your own banner and profile picture; persuasion by writing content; distribution by getting eyes on your profile and learning those tactics; supplement with courses or YouTube. You can write copy for your own sales page or promotions, create your own "minimum viable offer" and market it, or affiliate for a product to practice marketing. The skill stack from building a personal brand for a year transfers to almost any online business; with followers ("more than the average person"), your DMs are much more likely to be answered. "I wish I did this." He says he switched all the courses he offers because of this.
- **ejemplos:** SEO on own blog; own banner; minimum viable offer; affiliate marketing.
- **cita:** "the skill stack that you develop by building your own personal brand for a year can transition over into almost any online business"
- **terminos:** one-person business, personal brand, minimum viable offer

Then you can offer landing pages for $1,000–$2,000 to other creators. "All you have to do is just [ __ ] start."

"I don't want to hear that you don't want to start a business" — it's in your nature; "we weren't meant to be metaphorical monkeys in cubicles that are neurobiologically deprived of what we are wired to do." He recommends (not the only path) starting a personal brand: YouTube channel or Twitter account; he recommends Twitter because it requires no editing or visual knowledge, "only requires that you know how to type on a keyboard." Purpose: something to tie learnings to and apply them; "if you're just learning and not doing anything with it you're not learning." A personal brand requires every skill an online business requires: marketing, communication, writing and iterating, understanding what humans deem valuable so they engage and spread your message; eventually you package a solution from your experience and sell it. "A personal brand is you just online."

## CLÚSTER C-T13b-35: Persuasive education (teach what you do)
- idea: El pilar "arguably the most important" de la marca: "persuasive education changes behavior". La objeción del principiante es "¿por qué alguien aprendería de mí si ya hay mil videos?"; las soluciones son enseñar con un lente nuevo y persuadir a quien aún no está interesado. A la crítica de que "la gente solo enseña lo que hace", el autor responde que eso es lo que el mercado quiere y lo único que alguien está cualificado para enseñar ("if you weren't teaching what you know, then you're a liar"). Justin Welsh creó dos productos de $150 simplemente porque sabía crecer una audiencia y sumó un flujo de $1M al año; incluso $10k al mes "is life-changing".
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: ninguna.
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-015-149
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Pillar three: "persuasive education changes behavior" — the beginner's objection "why would anyone learn from me?"
- **desarrollo:** "Arguably the most important" pillar. Most creators build education businesses teaching their skills and interests (you could try to be a celebrity or OnlyFans model, but "we're aiming for some form of positive impact and meaningful work"). Many creators already teach what you want to teach; any skill on YouTube has tons of videos. That trips up beginners: they don't believe people have a reason to follow them. Two solutions: teach through a new lens; persuade the non-interested.
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "persuasive education changes behavior"
- **terminos:** persuasive education, education businesses
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-015-138
- **fuente:** How To Build A Better Personal Brand Than 99% Of People.md, 17:29-18:18, 2025-06-08
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-009-156
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** "People just teach what they do" — that's what the market wants and the only thing people are qualified to teach
- **desarrollo:** Last objection: "people are just teaching others what they do themselves." "No shit. That's what the market wants and that's what people are qualified to teach. That's arguably the only thing that people are qualified to teach you. If you weren't teaching what you know, then you're a liar." Dan teaches writing positioned for social media because that's what people find most valuable; he could teach writing unrelated to social media, but the creator economy is so large that's what everyone is doing.
- **ejemplos:** Dan's writing positioned for social media.
- **cita:** "If you weren't teaching what you know, then you're a liar"
- **terminos:** ninguno
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-009-155
- **fuente:** A Full Guide To Making Your First Profitable Product (Beginners, Take Notes).md, 13:12-14:10, 2025-02-10
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-009-037
- **tipo:** caso
- **titulo:** Justin Welsh: two $150 products based on what he did, adding a $1M/year revenue stream
- **desarrollo:** Justin Welsh has two $150 products based on the things he has done; he "literally just created a product because he knew how to grow an audience," adding "another million dollar a year Revenue stream." Dan qualifies: that's at Justin Welsh's level, but even a $10K/month revenue stream "is life-changing." Lesson: build an audience, learn about monetization along the way, teach what you learn, create a product or service, promote.
- **ejemplos:** Justin Welsh.
- **cita:** "even people that get like a 10K a month Revenue stream that's life-changing"
- **terminos:** ninguno
- **origen:** de-tercero:Justin Welsh
- **nivel:** complementario
- **prerrequisitos:** U-009-036

- **tension:** ninguna

### EV-104 — ¿Cuánto tarda aprender? Plazos y cifras de competencia
- **Antes:** "give yourself 20 years, not two weeks" (U-013-070, 2023-01-08) y el primer salto exponencial hacia el sexto mes (U-013-068, mismo video); comprometerse al menos tres meses (U-025-037, 2023-01-12); reemplazar el ingreso "within a year ish" con una hora al día (U-027-128, 2023-08-23); construir el "natural physique" unos 10 años antes de los business steroids (U-021-022, 2023-09-24); 1–5 años de educación y construcción (U-018-141, 2023-11-19); top 10% en 6–12 meses (U-017-189, 2024-01-18) → top 5% en 6–12 meses (U-013-178, 2024-03-24).
- **Después:** "6 years of results in 6 months" (U-020-102, 2024-02-11; U-023-189, 2024-07-21); "you can learn anything in two weeks" y "2 weeks vs 6 months" (U-021-109, 2025-03-09; U-020-163, U-016-252, 2025-08-10); una canción de guitarra en ~2 semanas y benchear 315 lb en ~3 años (U-020-170, U-020-181, 2025-08-10); de nuevo "2 to 5 years" para dominar marca, contenido, marketing y ventas incluso con IA (U-016-271, 2026-03-15).
- **Cambio exacto:** el énfasis pasa de la paciencia a la velocidad de aprendizaje y en 2026 vuelve a un plazo de años; las cifras no se reconcilian.
- **Razón que da el autor:** en 2025 aclara que "dos semanas" significa progreso sustancial hacia una meta, no maestría ("obviously that takes like 10 years"); U-016-252 registra el contraste con otros videos donde dice "it's going to take a few years".
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Sí: escalas distintas (técnica, habilidad, negocio, maestría).
- **Tipo:** cambio de énfasis (con inconsistencia de datos).


### EV-106 — Recordar lo que se lee frente a cambiar la mente
- **Antes:** "Learn New Skills FAST (How I Remember Everything I Learn)" (2022-11-27): retener mediante intención, curiosidad y reducción del execution gap (U-020-003, U-020-007).
- **Después:** "It's not about what information you have... It's about changing your mind" y "the purpose for reading is behavior change through identity change" (U-021-145, U-021-160, 2025-04-20); "If you need to remember it, it's not important" (U-021-192, 2026-08-09).

- **Razón que da el autor:** no la da explícitamente.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Son compatibles si la retención de 2022 se lee como consecuencia de la práctica.

### EV-107 — Tutoriales: saltarlos, reducirlos o usarlos en el punto de atasco
- **Antes:** "tutorial hell" (U-020-018, 2022-11-27); estudiar los fundamentals, luego un proyecto real, y comprar cursos o coaching al chocar con un problema (U-010-077, 2023-04-30); "supplement with tutorials to filter bad form" (U-009-103, 2023); con un problema en la cabeza se retiene lo que se ve (U-027-164, 2023-08-30); comprar primero un curso principiante y ver videos de fundamentos (U-020-109, 2024-02-11; U-023-194, 2024-07-21).
- **Después:** "courses are extremely valuable once you have the project" (U-027-236, 2025-01-12); "skip the tutorial phase, outline the project first", autocorregido en el mismo pasaje: "you don't necessarily need to skip tutorials, you just need to start the project and hit that struggling point" (U-021-115, 2025-03-09); "Create a tutorial phase" una vez que se juega (U-023-267, 2025-05-08); "skip the tutorial phase, outline a project first" (U-018-068, 2025-10-26); "don't watch 20 tutorials beforehand" y "publish the project" (U-021-194, 2026-08-09, reformulación de un tweet de 2025, U-021-121). En 2025–2026 la IA ocupa el lugar del tutorial en el momento del bloqueo (U-021-123, U-008-191).

- **Antes:** compra cualquier libro de marketing o persuasión y sigue la madriguera (U-011-064, 2022-06-25); para negocios, cursos sobre libros (U-020-188, 2022); "purchase one to three books on the topic and devour them" (U-024-053, 2023-05-19); "reading books is not learning, watching tutorials is not learning" (U-020-075, 2023-12-17); "buy three books… burn through them" (U-023-198, 2024-07-21), pero "reading a business book without having a business is kind of dumb", mientras la filosofía sirve siempre (U-023-192, 2024-07-21); la lectura es cómo adquieres conocimiento y absorbes años de experiencia (U-020-122, U-020-143, 2024-08-04).

## 6. Pensamiento


### EV-164 — De "start a personal brand" a "the death of the personal brand" (y su recuperación)
- **Antes:** "starting a personal brand nowadays is non-negotiable" (U-011-071, 2022-06-25); la marca personal es "Dynamic" (U-020-034, 2022-11); "it is the future" y no tiene sentido no empezarla (U-010-007, U-010-008, 2023-01-29); recomienda marca personal frente a ads (U-015-015, 2023-02); "the new wave that won't die out" (U-009-043, 2023-09-17).
- **Intermedio:** "The Death Of The Personal Brand" (2024-10-06): "personal brands are no longer personal"; arquetipo obsesionado con el dinero, glorified search engines, content creators frente a reality creators; influencer, creator y personal brand cargan connotaciones negativas; "don't be a personal brand... be you, in a place where your work can be discovered" (U-015-036, U-015-040, U-015-041, U-015-043, U-015-049, U-015-051), y se reconoce parcialmente culpable (U-015-047); rechazo del término "content creator" (U-006-059, 2024-12).
- **Después:** lo repite y reconoce "even though I just said that" (U-010-306, 2026-01); "get over" el cringe de la palabra (U-016-277, 2026-03-15); "it is the future in many ways, especially as a value creator" (U-022-127, 2026-08-19).
- **Cambio exacto:** no abandona la práctica (publicar, construir audiencia) sino la etiqueta y el modelo "cliché"; critica una deriva (la marca como funnel vacío) y en 2026 la recupera con el matiz del value creator.
- **Razón que da el autor:** la degradación del término, la saturación de plantillas, el modo supervivencia de los creadores y las prácticas de influencers y del direct response (U-014-148, U-016-129).
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Sí: la crítica apunta a la marca orientada solo al dinero; la recomendación, a la marca como expresión de curiosidad y valor. U-015-049 marca además tensión con su recomendación de elegir un nicho en otros videos, y U-010-306 la anota como matiz de U-010-007 y U-010-085.
- **Tipo:** cambio de énfasis.


### EV-187 — Shiny object syndrome: síntoma, señal o etiqueta; y sus siete fracasos
- **Antes:** "People warn against shiny object syndrome, but… never seen it be the case"; hay que probarlo todo (U-007-119, 2022-06; su tensión remite a U-007-102, "focus long enough to judge interest"); distingue buena y mala shiny object syndrome (U-013-034, U-013-066, 2023-01-08); "I attribute my unique success to shiny object syndrome" (U-016-220, 2023-08-01); no llamar shiny object syndrome a la experimentación (U-026-142, 2024-03-10); fracasos que apilaron habilidades irreemplazables (U-012-054, 2024-05-19). En sentido contrario: los siete fracasos como "una gran trampa", por ser "the architect of the business" (U-016-021, 2024-03-31); "the endless shiny object syndrome you have with finding a niche is a blatant sign that business hasn't clicked for you yet" (U-010-161, 2024-08-25).
- **Después:** critica a padres, profesores y amigos que dicen "shiny object syndrome is bad" para descalificar los múltiples intereses (U-010-217, 2025-02-23); cada interés deja un residuo y "your shiny object syndrome has been trying to tell you this whole time" que la especialización frena tu desarrollo (U-010-282, 2026-01; su tensión remite a U-010-006 y U-010-161); los negocios fallaron "thanks to shiny object syndrome", aunque le dejaron habilidades (U-004-087, 2026-05-14); eran "hobbies", no negocios (U-022-106, 2026-08-19).
- **Cambio exacto:** la misma etiqueta se usa como defensa, como diagnóstico negativo y como arma social que se rechaza; la defensa se vuelve más teórica (complejidad del modelo de la realidad).
- **Razón que da el autor:** no la explicita; la tensión de U-010-217 señala que en 2025 lo criticado es el uso de la etiqueta contra los múltiples intereses.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Sí: saltar de nicho en nicho buscando el más rentable frente a explorar intereses; los fracasos fallaron como negocios (por no adquirir clientes) pero sirvieron como acumulación de habilidades.
- **Tipo:** contradicción no resuelta.


### EV-228 — El empleo: "worst outcome", esclavitud, peldaño o destino
- **Antes:** las habilidades que generan ingresos se pueden ejercer también en un empleo; el emprendimiento es un camino, "not the only one" (U-014-021, 2022-05-28); la escuela es "a blessing and a curse" (U-011-054, 2022). Si al perder el empleo no puedes pagar las cuentas eres "by definition a modern slave", y la habilidad de ganar dinero "solo" se cultiva emprendiendo (U-011-186, U-011-188, 2023-04-16); el título "a stepping stone at best... a financial chain at worst" (U-011-123, 2023-02-19); los 9-to-5 son stepping stones peligrosos para la psique, salvo para quienes son felices en ellos (U-017-061, 2023-06-18); "I'm not anti-nine to five" (U-016-183) y, en el mismo video, "95 percent of nine to five workers are slaves" (U-016-202), con el empleo como trampolín (U-016-196, 2023-08-01); "The Future Of Work Is Play" (2023-12-31): se condicionó a ver el 9-to-5 como "the bane of my existence", "the worst outcome", y minutos después: "I see 9 to five jobs as a stepping stone; my problem with them is that they breed complacency" (U-012-004, U-012-006).
- **Intermedio:** "the fastest way to ruin your life", escuela y empleo como "life of the masses" (U-026-120, 2024-03-10), y en el mismo video "I see schools and jobs as a stepping stone... not something to avoid or absolutely evil", aclarando que muchos le atribuyen la postura contraria (U-026-131); el empleador "controls your entire life" (U-016-018), matizado por "there's degrees to this" (U-016-019), con "entrepreneurship is the only path" al control total (2024-03-31); el empleo es un juego finito (U-026-193, 2024-05-30); con el 1% feliz y autónomo, "keep the job" (U-006-158, 2024-07-13); "jobs are an absolutely incredible stepping stone" (U-019-038, 2024-08-11); trabajo y descanso significativos "never ever" se encuentran en un empleo asignado, matizado por "not all jobs are bad" (U-019-006, 2024-08); con Devon Eriksen se admite que alguien tiene que hacer tareas que cualquiera puede hacer (U-006-052, 2024-12-08).
- **Después:** "entrepreneurship is the only logical option" para quien quiere control, pero "not never work a job" (U-012-137, 2025-02-16); no está en el bando de "all 9-to-5s are bad" (U-010-239, 2025-02-23); "a great stepping stone, but often a death sentence" (U-023-272, 2025-05-08); "do everything in your power to not get a job" (U-025-178, 2025-07-27); unirse a una startup o aspirar a un rol ejecutivo figuran entre las "things I will do" (U-016-246, 2025-08-03); los empleos son "very worthy stepping stones", pero la wage slavery es real (U-012-203, 2026-06-28).
- **Cambio exacto:** no hay giro cronológico; oscila el tono entre el rechazo total y el peldaño, con una tendencia de "único camino" (2023–2024) a admitir vías dentro de organizaciones (2025). La condena absoluta se presenta como creencia autocondicionada y el problema pasa a ser la permanencia y la complacencia.
- **Razón que da el autor:** las assignments dan dirección, pero el destino que prometen decepciona y hay que quitarse las ruedas de entrenamiento; el problema es la falta de desafío y de evolución personal, no el empleo en sí; habla a quienes tienen "that desire for more"; el "95%" es una impresión de observación.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Sí: el empleo como etapa de aprendizaje de habilidades (él trabajó en una agencia) y para el 1% que ya está bien, frente al empleo como destino permanente; el rechazo es su regla personal y su consejo para quien quiere autonomía.
- **Tipo:** contradicción no resuelta.


### EV-289 — Detalles variables del empleo y de la deuda
- **Antes:** salario de "$60,000–70,000" (U-007-111, 2022-06-18); "front-end web developer, $50–55k" (U-013-001, 2022-08-28); préstamo del padre de $3.000 para las gafas de luz azul (U-007-109, 2022-06-18) frente a $2.000 (U-026-235, 2023-02-26).
- **Después:** "entry-level web designer, $55,000" (U-013-133, 2024-01-28); "$2,000 in loans even with a full-ride scholarship" (U-017-179, 2024-01-18) frente a $20.000 en préstamos estudiantiles (U-016-041, 2024-05-26; U-004-088, 2026-05-14) y $8.000 de deuda total como estudiante de tercer año (U-015-132, 2025-06-08); préstamo del padre de $2.000 (U-015-132).
- **Cambio exacto:** variaciones de memoria del relato; los $2.000 de U-017-179 podrían ser un error de transcripción frente a los $20.000 (no se puede confirmar).
- **Razón que da el autor:** no la da.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** No aplica.
- **Tipo:** inconsistencia de datos.


### EV-290 — La lista de los siete negocios fallidos
- **Antes:** YouTube de fitness, segundo canal, fotografía/arte, agencia de Facebook ads, dropshipping rave, gafas de luz azul, carteras, más agencias de contenido y SEO (U-007-097 a U-007-111, 2022-06-18); duración de 4–5 años (U-011-007, 2022-05-21).
- **Después:** 2–3 años (U-026-142, 2024-03-10); añade imprenta, "2-hour agency" de drop servicing y agencia de videos animados (U-014-135, 2024-10-20); casi 5 años de "beginner hell" y agencia de Facebook ads y de contenido SEO (U-015-088, 2025-02-02) frente a agencia SEO y de content marketing (U-015-132, 2025-06-08); arte digital, fotografía, agencias, dropshipping dos veces y un e-commerce (U-004-087, 2026-05-14); Facebook ads, SEO, dropshipping, diseño web, arte digital "y algunos más" (U-022-106, 2026-08-19).
- **Cambio exacto:** el número "siete" es estable; la composición y la duración, no.
- **Razón que da el autor:** no la da.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** No aplica.
- **Tipo:** inconsistencia de datos.


### EV-298 — Cuándo empezó en el gimnasio y cuánto tiempo estuvo sin Twitter
- **Antes / Después:** gimnasio: tras su primera ruptura (U-023-064, 2023-01-15; U-003-134, 2024-03-21), en octavo grado con el profesor Mr. Schroeder (U-008-078, 2024-01-21), desde los 15 años (U-019-091, 2024-06-16), en secundaria con Nutrient Timing (U-018-116, 2025-08-24). Twitter: borrado "for like 10 years" desde 2011 (U-015-004, 2023-02-12) frente a "about eight years" (U-010-100, 2023-07-04).
- **Cambio exacto:** variaciones narrativas compatibles.
- **Razón que da el autor:** no la da.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** No aplica.
- **Tipo:** inconsistencia de datos.

- **tipo:** término acuñado
- **definición:**
  - (2023–2026) Las habilidades se adquieren aprendiendo y apilando técnicas sobre algo específico.
  - (2023, 2025, forma "technique stacking") Adquirir habilidad = apilar técnicas.
- **acuñado por:** Dan Koe
- **fechas:** 2023 – 2026
- **IDs:** U-020-076, U-020-077, U-027-236, U-020-167, U-021-209, U-020-166, U-020-066
- **relación:** las dos formas (Stacking techniques / technique stacking) son la misma idea; vecino de skills are composed of techniques y de don't learn skills, acquire techniques.


### Start then learn
- **tipo:** término acuñado
- **definición:** Empezar el proyecto, construir con lo que sabes y aprender solo al toparse con un problema específico (formas "Start then learn / Start, build, learn / Build then learn").
- **acuñado por:** Dan Koe
- **fechas:** 2022 – 2024
- **IDs:** U-002-027, U-007-087, U-020-016, U-026-111, U-024-096, U-024-192, U-027-164
- **relación:** tres nombres para la misma práctica; vecino de rapid building y tutorial hell; ver Start with the purpose y Shitty rod.


### Start with the purpose
- **tipo:** término acuñado
- **definición:** Empezar por lo que quieres lograr (tocar una canción) y aprender solo la técnica del siguiente paso.
- **acuñado por:** Dan Koe
- **fechas:** 2025-08 (única aparición)
- **IDs:** U-020-170
- **relación:** vecino de just do things; ver Start then learn.


### starving artist
- **tipo:** palabra común con sentido propio
- **definición:**
  - (2022-12, 2025-02) Quien se apega a su versión del valor, ignora el negocio y no puede sostener su obra.
  - (2024) Quien domina una habilidad sin saber monetizarla.
  - (2023-04) Quien hace lo que ama sin estudiar psicología, marketing y ventas.
  - (2024-05) Creativo que hace lo que ama sin estudiar psicología, comportamiento humano e internet.
  - (2026, "starving artists") Quienes no integran arte y negocio; "time does not equate to value".
  - (2024, "Starving Artists") Quienes crean lo que creen valioso y se apegan a su versión del valor sin mirar al mercado.
- **acuñado por:** uso común, adoptado por Dan Koe (con sentido propio)
- **fechas:** 2022-12 – 2026
- **IDs:** U-007-176, U-015-092, U-015-069, U-011-155, U-016-040, U-022-132, U-001-113
- **relación:** variantes singular/plural; vecino de build in public y value creation; ver starving artist phase y starving market.


### Struggling point / hit a wall
- **tipo:** término acuñado
- **definición:** Momento del proyecto en que no puedes avanzar y la mente queda preparada para aprender.
- **acuñado por:** Dan Koe
- **fechas:** 2023 – 2025
- **IDs:** U-021-115, U-013-064, U-020-101
- **relación:** vecino de specific knowledge.


### Survive a project
- **tipo:** término acuñado
- **definición:** Cambio de conducta por el que intentas sobrevivir un proyecto, meta estructurada que da claridad.
- **acuñado por:** Dan Koe
- **fechas:** 2023-03 (única aparición)
- **IDs:** U-002-027
- **relación:** vecino de start then learn.


### survive the concept of self
- **tipo:** término acuñado
- **definición:** Uso moderno del mecanismo de supervivencia sobre la propia identidad.
- **acuñado por:** Dan Koe
- **fechas:** 2022 (única aparición)
- **IDs:** U-020-013
- **relación:** vecino de conceptual survival (T02); ver survive on a conceptual level.


### tactic collection / fake progress / false certainty
- **tipo:** término acuñado
- **definición:** Consumir tácticas sin parar da una falsa certeza de progreso, peor que el doom-scrolling.
- **acuñado por:** Dan Koe
- **fechas:** 2026 (única aparición)
- **IDs:** U-022-104
- **relación:** vecino de Brain rot.


### teach-learn loop
- **tipo:** término de invitado
- **definición:** Enseñar rápido, ver dónde se confunde el otro, estudiar, repetir.
- **acuñado por:** Sahil Bloom
- **fechas:** 2025 (única aparición)
- **IDs:** U-005-103
- **relación:** vecino de Feynman technique.


### the architect of the business
- **tipo:** término acuñado
- **definición:** La trampa de construir web, logo y LLC en vez de conseguir clientes.
- **acuñado por:** Dan Koe
- **fechas:** 2024 (única aparición)
- **IDs:** U-016-021
- **relación:** vecino de U-013-011 y U-022-106.


### The Meta perspective / big picture
- **tipo:** término acuñado
- **definición:** Obsesionarse con el panorama completo antes que con los detalles técnicos.
- **acuñado por:** Dan Koe
- **fechas:** 2022 – 2024
- **IDs:** U-011-098, U-001-059, U-012-055
- **relación:** vecino de entire puzzle.


### turning you into the project
- **tipo:** término acuñado
- **definición:** Tratar la vida entera como proyecto.
- **acuñado por:** Dan Koe
- **fechas:** 2022 (única aparición)
- **IDs:** U-020-019
- **relación:** vecino de your life is the project (U-023-192).


### tutorial hell
- **tipo:** término acuñado
- **definición:**
  - (2022–2025, "Tutorial hell") Quedar atrapado viendo tutoriales y acumulando conocimiento inútil como "brain fog" sin construir.
  - (2026) Aprender y ver tutoriales sin fin, con la dopamina de sentirse listo, sin nada que mostrar.
  - (2023–2026, forma "tutorial hell / tutorial phase") Acumular tutoriales antes de construir.
- **acuñado por:** Dan Koe (uso propio)
- **fechas:** 2022 – 2026
- **IDs:** U-020-018, U-020-101, U-023-189, U-027-164, U-003-043, U-003-248, U-012-055, U-010-088, U-027-236, U-010-264, U-022-106, U-010-077, U-018-068, U-008-181
- **relación:** vecino de brain fog, tutorial phase, beginner hell y just-in-case learning; ver Start then learn.


### tutorial phase
- **tipo:** término acuñado
- **definición:**
  - (2023) Fase inicial que no debe abandonarse ("1% of 50 years is 6 months").
  - (2025, "Tutorial phase") Fase de tutoriales que, hecha después de empezar y atascarse, sí produce aprendizaje.
  - (2025-07) Los 20 como preparación, no el juego principal.
  - (2025, forma "tutorial phase / game makers / boss fight") Estancarse en el nivel 10 porque a los creadores del juego no les conviene que subas; la batalla mayor es seguir tu camino.
  - (2024-01, forma "tutorial phase / locked quest") Las metas superiores están bloqueadas hasta tener la habilidad.
- **acuñado por:** Dan Koe
- **fechas:** 2023 – 2025
- **IDs:** U-009-110, U-011-145, U-021-115, U-023-267, U-025-172, U-013-220, U-025-167, U-025-069
- **relación:** vecino de struggling point, repeat the same 6 months, quests; ver tutorial hell. El sentido de aprendizaje y el sentido de "vida como videojuego" conviven en este término.


### Vessels (for learning the softer skills)
- **tipo:** término acuñado
- **definición:** Marketing and sales, writing and thinking, entrepreneurship: habilidades a través de las cuales se practican las liberating arts.
- **acuñado por:** Dan Koe
- **fechas:** 2024-07 (única aparición)
- **IDs:** U-006-090
- **relación:** vecino de futureproof skills y Evergreen; ver Seven Liberal Arts of the modern world / liberating arts y vessel.


### Zeigarnik effect squared
- **tipo:** término de tercero usado por el autor
- **definición:** Empezar tareas fáciles previas para que queden inconclusas y arrastren al trabajo principal.
- **acuñado por:** Justin Sung (adaptado)
- **fechas:** 2025-03 (única aparición)
- **IDs:** U-021-124
- **relación:** variante de Zeigarnik effect.
